O(q/\sigma)<2^{n^{\theta}}
for some
0<\theta<1
.

•

\sigma^{\prime}/\sigma=2^{\omega(\log\lambda)}

•

\sigma^{\prime}\leq q/(C\sqrt{nm\log q})
where
C
is the constant in
Lemma

A.4
.

•

\tau\geq\sqrt{qm}\log^{2}m

•

m^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\tau\leq q/8

For example, we can choose the parameters as follows (for sufficiently large
\lambda
):

n=\lambda^{c},\quad m=3n^{2},\quad q\approx 2^{\lambda},\quad\sigma=n^{2}\quad\sigma^{\prime}=2^{\lambda/6},\quad\tau=2^{2\lambda/3}.

(224)

where
c>1
is some constant.

Security

Theorem A.6.

If
\mathrm{LWE}_{n,m,q,D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma}}
is quantumly hard,
then the above scheme satisfies the requirements of special dual-mode OT (
Definition

A.1
)

Evaluation correctness:

For any
b\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathsf{mode}\in\{1,2\}
, and
(z_{0},z_{1})\in\{0,1\}^{2}
,
suppose that
(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{td})\leftarrow\mathsf{CRSGen}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{mode})
,

(\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},\mathsf{st})\leftarrow\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{crs},b)
,

\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}\leftarrow\mathsf{Send}(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},(z_{0},z_{1}))
.
Using the notations in the description of the scheme, we have

\displaystyle\left|w_{b,n+1}-\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{b,[1,n]}\mod q\right|

(225)

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}\mathbf{t}_{b}+z_{b}\lfloor q/2\rfloor-\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{t}_{b}\mod q\right|

(226)

\displaystyle=\left|(\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top})\mathbf{t}_{b}+z_{b}\lfloor q/2\rfloor-\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{t}_{b}\mod q\right|

(227)

\displaystyle=\left|{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{t}_{b}+z_{b}\lfloor q/2\rfloor\mod q\right|

(228)

Since
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
is sampled from
D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{t}
is sampled from
D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\tau}
, we have
\|\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{m}\sigma^{\prime}
and
\|\mathbf{t}_{b}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{m}\tau
.
Thus,

\|{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{t}_{b}\|_{\infty}\leq m^{2}\sigma^{\prime}\tau\leq q/8
.
Thus, when
z_{b}=0
, we have

\left|w_{b,n+1}-\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{b,[1,n]}\mod q\right|\leq q/8<q/4.

Similarly,
when
z_{b}=0
, we have

\left|w_{b,n+1}-\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{w}_{b,[1,n]}\mod q\right|\geq\lfloor q/2\rfloor-q/8>q/4.

The above together imply the evaluation correctness.

Unique state:

Let

\mathsf{mode}\in\{1,2\}
,

(\mathsf{crs}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{v}),\mathsf{td}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{v}))\in\mathrm{Supp}(\mathsf{CRSGen}(1^{\lambda},\mathsf{mode}))
,
b\in\{0,1\}
, and
(\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathsf{st}=\mathbf{r})\in\mathrm{Supp}(\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{crs},b))
.
Then we have
\mathbf{u}_{0}=\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}-b\mathbf{v}^{\top}
for some
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
such that
\|\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma^{\prime}\leq q/(C\sqrt{nm\log q})
.
By
Lemma

A.4
, there is a PPT algorithm that recovers
\mathsf{st}=\mathbf{r}
from
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{T}
,
b
,
\mathbf{v}
, and
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
. This implies the uniqueness of the state.

Mode indistinguishability:

The only difference between the two modes is that
\mathbf{v}
is uniformly random when
\mathsf{mode}=1
and is an LWE instance when
\mathsf{mode}=2
. Thus, the mode indistinguishability directly follows from the assumed quantum hardness of
\mathrm{LWE}_{n,m,q,D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma}}
.

Statistical security against malicious senders in the hiding mode:

Fix
\mathsf{crs}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{v})
in the support of
\mathsf{CRSGen}(1^{\lambda},2)
.
It satisfies
\mathbf{v}^{\top}=\mathbf{s}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{e}^{\top}
for some
\mathbf{s}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
and

\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}
such that
\|\mathbf{e}\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma
.
Suppose
(\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{b},\mathsf{st})\leftarrow\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{crs},b)
for
b\in\{0,1\}
.
Then we can write

\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{0}=\mathbf{r}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}

and

\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{1}=\mathbf{r}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\mathbf{v}=(\mathbf{r}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{s}^{\top})\mathbf{A}+({\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}^{\top})

for

\mathbf{r}_{0},\mathbf{r}_{1}\leftarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}

and
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}\leftarrow D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}
.
Since
\mathbf{r}_{1}^{\top}
is uniformly random,
(\mathbf{r}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{s}^{\top})
is also uniformly random. Moreover,
since
\|\mathbf{e}\|\leq\sqrt{m}\sigma
,
the statistical distance between the distributions of
{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}^{\top}
and
({\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}^{\top})
is at most

\sqrt{2\left(1-\exp\left(-2\pi m\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}\right)\right)}\leq\sqrt{4\pi m\sigma/\sigma^{\prime}}={\mathsf{negl}}(\lambda)

by
Lemma

A.2

and
\sigma^{\prime}/\sigma=2^{\omega(\log\lambda)}
. Thus, the distributions of
\mathsf{msg}_{0}^{(1)}
and
\mathsf{msg}_{1}^{(1)}
are statistically negligibly close in
\lambda
.

State recoverability in the hiding mode:

Let
\mathsf{StaRcv}
be an algorithm that works as follows.

\mathsf{StaRcv}(\mathsf{td},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)})
:

On input
\mathsf{td}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{v})
and
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
,
compute
\mathbf{u}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{v}
,
run
(\mathbf{r}_{b},\mathbf{e}_{b}^{\prime})\leftarrow\mathsf{Invert}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{u}_{b})
for
b\in\{0,1\}
and output
(\mathbf{r}_{0},\mathbf{r}_{1})
.

Suppose
(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{td})\leftarrow\mathsf{CRSGen}(1^{\lambda},2)
and

(\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},\mathsf{st})\leftarrow\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{crs},b)
.
Then
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0}=\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}-b\mathbf{v}
and
\mathsf{st}=\mathbf{r}
where
\mathbf{r}\leftarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}

and
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\leftarrow D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}
.
Then we have
\mathbf{u}_{b}^{\top}=\mathbf{u}_{0}^{\top}+b\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}
.
Thus, for
(\mathbf{r}_{0},\mathbf{r}_{1})
output by
\mathsf{StaRcv}(\mathsf{td},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)})
, we have
\mathbf{r}_{b}=\mathbf{r}
by
Lemma

A.4
and
\|\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\|\leq\sqrt{m}\sigma^{\prime}\leq q/(C\sqrt{n\log q})
.

Extractability in the extraction mode:

Let
\mathrm{Ext}
and
\mathsf{Sim}
be algorithms that work as follows.

\mathrm{Ext}(\mathsf{td},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)})
:

On input
\mathsf{td}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{v})
and
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
, compute
\mathbf{u}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{v}

and run
\beta_{b}\leftarrow\mathsf{IsMessy}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{u}_{b})
.
If
\beta_{0}=1
, output
1
, else if
\beta_{1}=1
, output
0
, otherwise output
\bot
.

\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},z_{b})
:

On input
\mathsf{crs}=(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{v})
,
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}=\mathbf{u}_{0}
, and
z_{b}\in\{0,1\}
,
Run
(\mathbf{w}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{1})\leftarrow\mathsf{Send}(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},(z_{b},z_{b}))
and output
(\mathbf{w}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{1})
.
Note that it uses the same
z_{b}
for generating both
\mathbf{w}_{0}
and
\mathbf{w}_{1}
.

Fix
(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T})
that satisfies the two items of
Lemma

A.5
.
Let
G\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}
be the subset in the first item of
Lemma

A.5
, that is, for
any
\mathbf{v}\notin G
,

\mathsf{IsMessy}(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{T},\mathbf{v})
returns
1
with overwhelming probability.
Since
G
consists of at most
(1/2\sqrt{q})^{m}
fraction of elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}
,
|G|\leq(\sqrt{q}/2)^{m}
.
Therefore, we have

\#\{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}:\exists\mathbf{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}\text{~s.t.~}\mathbf{u}_{0}\in G\land\mathbf{u}_{1}\in G\}\leq|G|^{2}\leq(q/4)^{m}

(229)

where
\mathbf{u}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{v}
.
Thus, we have

\Pr_{\mathbf{v}\leftarrow\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}}[\exists\mathbf{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m}\text{~s.t.~}\mathbf{u}_{0}\in G\land\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{v}\in G]\leq 4^{-m}.

(230)

Thus, when
\mathsf{td}
and
\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}
are honestly generated,
\mathrm{Ext}(\mathsf{td},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)})
returns a non-
\bot
bit with overwhelming probability.
By the second item of
Lemma

A.5
,
when
\mathrm{Ext}(\mathsf{td},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)})
returns
b\in\{0,1\}
,

(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{u}_{1-b})\in M_{\delta,\tau}
with overwhelming probability where
M_{\delta,\tau}
is as defined in
Lemma

A.5
.
Note that the only difference between
\mathsf{Send}(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},(z_{0},z_{1}))
and
\mathsf{Sim}(\mathsf{crs},\mathsf{msg}^{(1)},z_{b})
is that
\mathbf{w}_{1-b}
is generated using
z_{1-b}
in the former but using
z_{b}
in the latter.
By the definition of
M_{\delta,\tau}
, when
(\mathbf{A},\mathbf{u}_{1-b})\in M_{\delta,\tau}
, the distribution of
\mathbf{w}_{1-b}
generated in either way has statistical distance at most
\delta={\mathsf{negl}}(n)
from the uniform distribution.
Thus, the above implies extractability in the extraction mode.

Efficient state superposition:

It suffices to show that
there is a QPT algorithm that is given
b\in\{0,1\}
,
\mathbf{A}
, and
\mathbf{v}
, and generates a state that is within negligible trace distance of the following state:

\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}})}\sqrt{p_{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}}\ket{b}\ket{\vr}\ket{\vr^\trans\mA+{\ve^\prime}^\trans+(-1)^b\vv^\trans}~

(231)

where
p_{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}
is the probability that
\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{crs},b)
chooses the randomness
(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
.

This can be done as follows. First, as shown in
[
Reg09
,
BCM+21
]
, the Grover-Rudolph algorithm
[
GR02
]
enables us to generate the following Gaussian superposition up to negligible error:

\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}})}\sqrt{D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathbf{e}^{\prime})}\ket{\ve^\prime}.

(232)

We prepare
\ket{b}
in another register and
generate
a uniform superposition over
\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}
in yet another register. Then, the whole system can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\ket{b}\otimes q^{-n/2}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n}}\ket{\vr}\otimes\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}})}\sqrt{D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathbf{e}^{\prime})}\ket{\ve^\prime}

(233)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}})}\sqrt{p_{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}}\ket{b}\ket{\vr}\ket{\ve^\prime}

(234)

where the equality follows from
p_{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}=q^{-n}D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathbf{e}^{\prime})
.

By using
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{v}
, we can coherently compute
\mathbf{r}^{\top}\mathbf{A}+{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}^{\top}+(-1)^{b}\mathbf{v}^{\top}
in another register. This yields the following state:

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{n},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(D_{\mathbb{Z}_{q}^{m},\sigma^{\prime}})}\sqrt{p_{\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}}\ket{b}\ket{\vr}\ket{\ve^\prime}\ket{\vr^\trans\mA+{\ve^\prime}^\trans+(-1)^b\vv^\trans}.

(235)

Finally, uncomputing the third register results in the desired state.

A.3
From OT to SFE via Garbled Circuit

We explain how to transform special dual-mode OT into special dual-mode SFE using garbled circuit.
We only provide a sketch since the technique is fairly standard.

Garbled circuit.

We first quickly review the notion of garbled circuit.
A circuit garbling scheme
\mathsf{GC}
for circuits is a tuple
(\mathsf{Grbl},\mathsf{Eval})
of two PPT algorithms with the following syntax.

•

A garbling algorithm
\mathsf{Grbl}
, given a security parameter
1^{\lambda}
and a circuit
C
with
n
-bit input, outputs a garbled circuit
\widetilde{C}
and input labels
\{\mathsf{lab}_{i,b}\}_{i\in[n],b\in\{0,1\}}
.

•

The evaluation algorithm, given a garbled circuit
\widetilde{C}
and
n
input labels
\{\mathsf{lab}_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
,
outputs
y
.

As correctness, we require
\mathsf{Eval}({\widetilde{C}},\{\mathsf{lab}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]})=C(x)
holds for every circuit
C
with
n
-bit input and
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,
where
({\widetilde{C}},\{\mathsf{lab}_{i,b}\}_{i\in[n],b\in\{0,1\}})\allowbreak\leftarrow\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},C)
.
As security, we require that for any circuit
C
and input
x
,
({\widetilde{C}},\{\mathsf{lab}_{i,x[i]}\}_{i\in[n]})
can be simulated only from
C(x)
in the computationally indistinguishable way.
A circuit garbling scheme can be realized based on OWFs
[
Yao86
]
.

Transformation based on garbled circuit.

We now sketch how to transform a special dual-mode OT scheme
\mathsf{OT}=\mathsf{OT}.(\mathsf{CRSGen},\mathsf{Rec}_{1},\allowbreak\mathsf{Send},\mathsf{Rec}_{2})
into a special dual-mode SFE scheme
\mathsf{SFE}=\mathsf{SFE}.(\mathsf{CRSGen},\mathsf{Rec}_{1},\allowbreak\mathsf{Send},\mathsf{Rec}_{2})
using a circuit garbling scheme
\mathsf{GC}=(\mathsf{Grbl},\mathsf{Eval})
.

\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{CRSGen}
:

It runs
\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{CRSGen}

n
-times and gets
(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i},\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{td}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
. It sets
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{crs}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{td}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{td}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
.

\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{Rec}_{1}
:

Given
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{crs}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
and
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
, it executes
(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{i},\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{st}_{i})\leftarrow\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{Rec}_{1}(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i},x[i])
for every
i\in[n]
and outputs
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
and
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{st}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{st}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
.

\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{Send}
:

Given
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{crs}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
,
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
, and a circuit
C
, it first executes
(\widetilde{C},(\mathsf{lab}_{i,b})_{i\in[n],b\in\{0,1\}})\leftarrow\mathsf{Grbl}(1^{\lambda},C)
. It then runs
\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{Send}(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i},\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(1)}_{i},(\mathsf{lab}_{i,0},\mathsf{lab}_{i,1}))
for every
i\in[n]
and outputs
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}:=(\widetilde{C},(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}_{i})_{i\in[n]})
.

\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{Rec}_{2}
:

Given
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{crs}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
,
x\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{st}:=(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{st}_{i})_{i\in[n]}
, and
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}:=(\widetilde{C},(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}_{i})_{i\in[n]})
, it executes
\mathsf{lab}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{Rec}_{2}(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{crs}_{i},x[i],\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{st}_{i},\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}_{i})
for every
i\in[n]
and outputs
y\leftarrow\mathsf{Eval}(\widetilde{C},(\mathsf{lab}_{i})_{i\in[n]})
.

We can easily check that the evaluation correctness of
\mathsf{SFE}
follows from that of
\mathsf{OT}
and
\mathsf{GC}
.

\mathsf{SFE}
uses
n
instances of
\mathsf{OT}
in parallel and uses
\mathsf{GC}
on top of it.
Especially, the CRS, the trapdoor, and the first message of
\mathsf{SFE}
consist of just
n
copies of those of
\mathsf{OT}
.
By this construction, the unique state property, mode indistinguishability, statistical security against malicious senders in the hiding mode, state recoverability in the hiding mode, and efficient state superposition property of
\mathsf{SFE}
directly follow from those of
\mathsf{OT}
, respectively.
Finally, we can prove the extractability in the extraction mode of
\mathsf{SFE}
based on that of
\mathsf{OT}
and the security of
\mathsf{GC}
.
Roughly speaking, the sender message
\mathsf{SFE}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}
of
\mathsf{SFE}
is composed of
(\widetilde{C},(\mathsf{OT}.\mathsf{msg}^{(2)}_{i})_{i\in[n]})
, and the first component can be simulated from
C(x)
by the security of
\mathsf{GC}
and the second one can be simulated by the the extractability in the extraction mode of
\mathsf{OT}
together with the simulated labels of
\mathsf{GC}
.
